2025-06-17T20:40:25.8693475Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8693580Z <pr_title>
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8694033Z Simplify CI: consolidate bloated workflows and add immediate wheel access
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8694770Z </pr_title>
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8694973Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8695150Z <pr_description>
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8696594Z This PR addresses the CI complexity and reliability issues by dramatically simplifying the workflow structure and providing multiple access methods for package installation.
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8698137Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8698283Z ## Problem
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8698854Z The repository had **6 CI workflow files** with significant redundancy:
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8699607Z - `build-artifacts.yaml`
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8700098Z - `build-packages-dev.yaml` 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8700598Z - `build-packages-prod.yaml`
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8701104Z - `build-packages-staging.yaml`
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8701591Z - `create-release.yaml`
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8702001Z - `tools.yaml`
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8702221Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8703329Z This created maintenance complexity and CI reliability issues, making it difficult to successfully build and access wheel artifacts.
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8704356Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8704482Z ## Solution
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8704865Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8705022Z ### 1. Consolidated CI Workflows
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8705566Z **Reduced from 6 to 3 focused workflows** (50% reduction):
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8705999Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8706500Z - **`build-and-deploy.yaml`**: Unified workflow that builds once and deploys to appropriate environments based on triggers
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8707331Z - **`commit-wheels.yaml`**: Fallback workflow that commits wheels directly to the repository when CI builds succeed
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8708176Z - **`tools.yaml`**: Unchanged, handles build tools and container management
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8708477Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8708582Z ### 2. Multiple Installation Methods
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8708876Z Users can now install packages via:
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8709066Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8709160Z **One-line installer:**
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8709378Z ```bash
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8709886Z curl -sSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/install.sh | bash -s -- pandas
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8710468Z ```
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8710569Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8710660Z **Direct wheel download:**
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8710890Z ```bash
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8711537Z curl -L https://raw.githubusercontent.com/sgbaird/opentrons-python-packages/main/wheels/pandas-1.5.0-cp310-cp310-linux_armv7l.whl -o /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8712282Z pip install /tmp/pandas.whl
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8712515Z ```
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8712610Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8712763Z **CI artifacts and releases** (when CI runs successfully)
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8713013Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8713109Z ### 3. Immediate Package Access
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8713704Z Added pre-built wheel files directly to the repository in the `wheels/` directory, so packages are immediately available even if CI fails.
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8714230Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8714311Z ## Key Benefits
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8714573Z - **Simplified maintenance**: 50% fewer workflow files
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8715075Z - **Eliminated redundancy**: Single build logic instead of duplicated across multiple files
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8715601Z - **Improved reliability**: Multiple fallback access methods
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8716089Z - **Better user experience**: Immediate access to packages via curl/pip as requested
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8716665Z - **Clear documentation**: Added `CI-SIMPLIFICATION.md` explaining the changes
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8716982Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8717363Z The solution directly addresses the "bloated" CI concern while ensuring reliable package access through multiple channels.
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8718058Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8718169Z Fixes #11.
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8718287Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8718290Z

2025-06-17T20:40:25.8777007Z Based on the Tailscale OAuth documentation, here are the customized setup instructions for your GitHub Actions integration:
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8777488Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8777603Z ## Tailscale OAuth Client Setup Instructions
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8777938Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8778086Z ### 1. Create OAuth Client
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8778657Z 1. Go to the [OAuth clients page](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/settings/oauth) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8779195Z 2. Click **Generate OAuth client**
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8779673Z 3. Select the required scopes for GitHub Actions (recommend `auth_keys` scope for node registration)
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8780222Z 4. Add the `tag:ci` tag (or create a custom tag for GitHub Actions)
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8780586Z 5. Click **Generate client**
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8781076Z 6. **Important**: Copy both the Client ID and Client Secret immediately - you won't be able to view the secret again
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8781506Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8781598Z ### 2. Add GitHub Secrets
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8781884Z Add these to your repository's GitHub Actions secrets:
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8782235Z - `TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`: Your OAuth client ID
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8782553Z - `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`: Your OAuth client secret  
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8782766Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8782858Z ### 3. Repository Configuration
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8783846Z The `.github/workflows/copilot-setup-steps.yml` file is already configured with the Tailscale GitHub Action (currently commented out). Once you add the secrets above, I can uncomment and activate the Tailscale connection for OT-2 simulator access.
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8784757Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8784836Z ### 4. Verification
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8785539Z After setup, the workflow will automatically connect to your Tailscale network using the `tag:ci` tag, allowing secure access to your OT-2 simulator for testing Prefect installations.

2025-06-17T20:40:25.8791609Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8791891Z Based on the Tailscale documentation, here's how to update your ACLs to add the new `tag:ci` tag:
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8792267Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8792360Z ## ACL JSON Configuration
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8792504Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8792729Z Add the tag to your `tagOwners` section in the Tailscale admin console ACL editor:
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8793047Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8793130Z ```json
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8793314Z {
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8793489Z   "tagOwners": {
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8793710Z     "tag:ci": ["your-email@domain.com"]
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8794103Z   },
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8794283Z   "acls": [
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8794466Z     {
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8794647Z       "action": "accept",
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8794880Z       "src": ["tag:ci"],
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8795204Z       "dst": ["*:*"]
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8795406Z     }
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8795578Z   ]
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8795741Z }
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8795908Z ```
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8795998Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8796082Z ## Admin Console Steps
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8796229Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8796545Z 1. Go to [Access Controls](https://login.tailscale.com/admin/acls) in your Tailscale admin console
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8797057Z 2. Add the `tag:ci` entry to your `tagOwners` section 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8797565Z 3. Optionally add ACL rules for the tag (the example above allows `tag:ci` devices to access anything)
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8798140Z 4. Save the policy file
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8798289Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8798382Z ## OAuth Client Configuration
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8798548Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8799018Z The OAuth client you created should automatically work with any tags defined in your ACL. No additional admin console configuration is needed beyond:
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8799576Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8799795Z 1. Creating the OAuth client with appropriate scopes (`auth_keys` recommended)
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8800268Z 2. Adding the `tag:ci` tag to your ACL policy as shown above
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8800707Z 3. Adding the GitHub secrets (`TS_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` and `TS_OAUTH_SECRET`)
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8801004Z 
2025-06-17T20:40:25.8801534Z The Tailscale GitHub Action will use the OAuth client to authenticate and automatically apply the `tag:ci` tag to the GitHub Actions runner when it connects to your tailnet.
